    1| # Independent raw-axis qualification
    2| 
    3| This lane is separately authored from canonical definitions and generator logic. It binds raw filtered revision5 inputs, the independently frozen audit facts (`f097e5a541b279f2081c71f54ca3403151475348b8a30245e6846708590e293e`), pinned rates, and root policy approval07303d5c. Frozen originals are copied byte-for-byte in frozen-raw/. The test source imports only serde_json, sha2 and the Rust standard library; it imports no canonical semantic helper and calculates no full reducer result.
    4| 
    5| Run the `independent_raw_axes` Cargo integration test. `ORACLE_REPORT=<path>` optionally writes qualification.json. Every original173case has explicit raw-selection and full-C-K standing. The latter remains unestablished. Source/line assertions cover all137raw-selected cases (105current-producer,32future-profile),142sources,287rows,147lexical reservation groups;104observations have independently recomputed exact usage extrema. Future profile projection follows the frozen selection, never feeds it. The other2future-profile cases have no selected dollar source. Missing-launch dollar evidence is additionally covered as an unresolved control; it is not assigned an invented launch identity. New controls are independently checked outside the historical173case count.
    6| 
    7| Supported axes: exact raw/rate/fact hashes; source selection and source/line totality; maximum distinct well-formed reservation amounts; unique comparison applicability; provider-qualified identities and explicit declared family; billing/auxiliary field states; required counts; feasible OpenAI cache vertices; Anthropic lifetime/residual bounds; complete status200 AND successful provider terminal AND counts conjunction; error-status usage retention; malformed IDs; historical revision findings and writer arithmetic controls. Single-observation expected valuations are compared to separately recomputed raw arithmetic. Multi-observation reducer aggregation, complete correlation/transition semantics, full K findings and production gate authority remain unestablished.
    8| 
    9| qualification.json uses established/contradicted/unestablished standing. The assertions fail on contradictions; they do not rewrite expectations. A passing raw-axis check is not full C-K acceptance, a reference reducer, production integration, freeze approval or S2a readiness.
   10| 
   11| Future gate controls consume only a separate top-level trusted_fixture_inputs mapping with an exact payload digest, authority, session, policy reference and evidence reference. Outputs say test_mock. Producer writer labels, verified:true, provenance labels and self-supplied receipt digests cannot mint this input. This tests conditional policy and payload binding only. No production verifier, cryptography, authentication channel or enforcement is implemented.
   12| 
   13| Writer controls preserve the historical synthetic originals. R6-C01 supplies compatible local reservation configuration (input_bound1000, output_cap150, flex0.5): (1000*12.5+150*50)*0.5/1e6=.01. Its observed-default incomplete usage supports.015 and does not release the reservation. R6-C02 supplies input_bound6000/output_cap14500/default: R=.8. The pinned parser can log a non200 response.completed payload and usage; error_status charges0 while supported usage1USD is retained. These are local writer arithmetic/branch consistency witnesses, with reachability not_established. Passing a reservation lower-bound check never proves actual producer reachability.
   14| 
   15| Producer basis: released92a03789 pi_container.py SHA780837341790780f84163ee6151b8ce66cff56f5a3f6ddf8c15f939dba50f470; exact lifetime keys ephemeral_5m_input_tokens and ephemeral_1h_input_tokens at111–112,653–660,1046–1058; non200 parser path1510–1572. Graph evidence is the independent audit's Tier2 generation2026-11-13T21:53:10Z with unresolved operator-call coverage; material source ranges were read directly. This external subject is outside_project and has no claimed graph completeness.
   16| 
   17| Exact hypothetical body/config witnesses are in writer-controls.json. Rust assertions independently serialize those bodies, reconstruct byte input bounds with the pinned32token item overhead, and verify logged multiplier floors. During implementation, the first cloned R6-C01 witness incorrectly retained floor0 after requested-flex/observed-default settlement; pinned lines1007–1020 establish the raised floor1. The new control was corrected to1.0 and rechecked. The initial owned snapshot58f9f63d/archive30f71778 retains that transient defect for lineage; it is superseded. Historical173raw cases were never changed.
   18| 
   19| Revision7 independent delta: root-approved financial provisionality, raw Anthropic tier and split completeness guards, self-consistent semantic mutants and exact historical writer-floor contradictions are recorded in corpus/REVISION-7.md and REVISION-7.json. FROZEN-FACTS and173raw inputs remain untouched. The old fact candidate's status/terminal-only candidate completeness is not final policy: inconsistent lifetime splits remain incomplete. New assertions implement approved P2 and disclose full C-K and live authority as unestablished.

   27|   625| def anthropic_identity_refusal(event, model_id: str) -> str | None:
   28|   626|     """None when a stream's first event is a message_start naming exactly the
   29|   627|     authorized model; otherwise why the stream is refused. The response's own
   30|   628|     message.model is checked, not the request's."""

   33|   631|     message = event.get("message")
   34|   632|     if not isinstance(message, dict):
   35|   633|         return "response_model_malformed"
   36|   634|     if "model" not in message or message["model"] is None:
   37|   635|         return "response_model_missing"
   38|   636|     if not isinstance(message["model"], str) or not message["model"]:
   39|   637|         return "response_model_malformed"
   40|   638|     return None if message["model"] == model_id else "response_model_mismatch"

   44|   680| def usage_usd(rates: dict, *, input_tokens, cached, cache_write, output_tokens, multiplier) -> float:
   45|   681|     """Same formula as usage_ledger.codex_usd, times the tier multiplier."""
   46|   682|     long = input_tokens > rates["long_input_threshold"]
   47|   683|     m_in, m_out = (rates["long_input_multiplier"], rates["long_output_multiplier"]) if long else (1, 1)
   48|   684|     base = ((input_tokens - cached - cache_write) * rates["input"] + cached * rates["cached_input"]
   49|   685|             + cache_write * rates["cache_write"]) * m_in + output_tokens * rates["output"] * m_out
   50|   686|     return base / 1e6 * multiplier
   51|   687| 
   52|   688| 
   53|   689| def reserve_usd(rates: dict, *, input_bound: int, max_output_tokens: int, multiplier: float) -> float:
   54|   690|     """Worst case: every input token at the dearest input rate (a cache can always miss)."""
   55|   691|     long = input_bound > rates["long_input_threshold"]
   56|   692|     m_in, m_out = (rates["long_input_multiplier"], rates["long_output_multiplier"]) if long else (1, 1)
   57|   693|     dearest = max(rates["input"], rates["cache_write"])
   58|   694|     return (input_bound * dearest * m_in + max_output_tokens * rates["output"] * m_out) / 1e6 * multiplier
   59|   695|

   61|   697| def _canonical(value) -> bytes:
   62|   698|     return json.dumps(value, sort_keys=True, separators=(",", ":")).encode()
   63|   699| 
   64|   700| 
   65|   701| def input_basis(body: dict, items_key: str = "input") -> dict:
   66|   702|     """Byte sizes and a hash chain over the request's input items
   67|   703|     (`input` for Responses, `messages` for Anthropic Messages).
   68|   704| 
   69|   705|     chains[k] identifies the first k items, so a later request can be checked
   70|   706|     for starting with an earlier request's whole input. A token is at least
   71|   707|     one byte of its text and JSON escaping only adds bytes, so a canonical
   72|   708|     item's byte length bounds its text tokens.
   73|   709|     """
   74|   710|     items = body.get(items_key, [])
   75|   711|     if not isinstance(items, list):
   76|   712|         items = [items]
   77|   713|     rest = _canonical({k: v for k, v in body.items() if k != items_key})
   78|   714|     h = hashlib.sha256(b"").digest()
   79|   715|     chains, sizes = [h.hex()], []
   80|   716|     for item in items:
   81|   717|         raw = _canonical(item)
   82|   718|         sizes.append(len(raw))
   83|   719|         h = hashlib.sha256(h + hashlib.sha256(raw).digest()).digest()
   84|   720|         chains.append(h.hex())
   85|   721|     return {"items": len(items), "chains": chains, "item_bytes": sizes,
   86|   722|             "rest_sha256": hashlib.sha256(rest).hexdigest(), "rest_bytes": len(rest)}
   87|   723| 
   88|   724| 
   89|   725| def input_bound(basis: dict, prefix: dict | None) -> tuple[int, str]:
   90|   726|     """Upper bound on the request's input tokens, and how it was found.
   91|   727| 
   92|   728|     prefix: the provider's input_tokens for an earlier request whose input
   93|   729|     this request starts with. Those tokens already cover its instructions and
   94|   730|     tools; changed ones are counted again in full.
   95|   731|     """
   96|   732|     n, sizes = basis["items"], basis["item_bytes"]
   97|   733|     if prefix and isinstance(prefix.get("input_tokens"), int) and prefix["items"] <= n \
   98|   734|             and basis["chains"][prefix["items"]] == prefix["chain_sha256"]:
   99|   735|         k = prefix["items"]
  100|   736|         bound = prefix["input_tokens"] + sum(sizes[k:]) + ITEM_OVERHEAD_TOKENS * (n - k)
  101|   737|         if basis["rest_sha256"] != prefix["rest_sha256"]:
  102|   738|             bound += basis["rest_bytes"] + ITEM_OVERHEAD_TOKENS
  103|   739|         return bound, "prefix"
  104|   740|     return basis["rest_bytes"] + sum(sizes) + ITEM_OVERHEAD_TOKENS * (n + 1), "bytes"

  126| # ---- budgets: SubscriptionProxyBudget, UsdBudget, AnthropicUsdBudget: lines 868-1076 ----
  127|   868| class SubscriptionProxyBudget:
  128|   869|     """Serialized stopping threshold, with no reservation or hard output cap.
  129|   870| 
  130|   871|     The proxy owns locking and durably writes admission before any upstream effect.
  131|   872|     An unknown exchange keeps its admission unresolved and permanently stops this session.
  132|   873|     """
  133|   874|     max_output_tokens = None
  134|   875| 
  135|   876|     def __init__(self, *, max_usd, rates, spent_before_usd=0.0):
  136|   877|         self.max_usd, self.rates = max_usd, rates
  137|   878|         self.settled = spent_before_usd
  138|   879|         self.inflight = None
  139|   880|         self.exhausted = spent_before_usd >= max_usd
  140|   881|         self.unknown = False
  141|   882|         self.serial = 0
  142|   883| 
  143|   884|     def admit(self):
  144|   885|         if self.exhausted:
  145|   886|             return None, "usd_limit: subscription proxy threshold reached or accounting unknown"
  146|   887|         if self.inflight is not None:
  147|   888|             return None, "subscription_proxy_request_in_flight"
  148|   889|         self.serial += 1
  149|   890|         self.inflight = self.serial
  150|   891|         return {"rid": self.serial}, None
  151|   892| 
  152|   893|     def settle(self, ticket, *, http_status, response, terminal, stream_error):
  153|   894|         usage = response.get("usage")
  154|   895|         reason = None
  155|   896|         if (http_status != 200 or stream_error or terminal not in ("response.completed", "response.done")):
  156|   897|             reason = "exchange_not_completed"
  157|   898|         elif (response.get("model") != self.rates["model"] or not isinstance(response.get("id"), str)
  158|   899|               or not response["id"].strip()):
  159|   900|             reason = "response_identity_not_established"
  160|   901|         elif response.get("status") not in (None, "completed") or response.get("error"):
  161|   902|             reason = "exchange_not_completed"
  162|   903|         elif not isinstance(usage, dict):
  163|   904|             reason = "usage_not_established"
  164|   905|         else:
  165|   906|             counts = [usage.get(k) for k in ("input_tokens", "output_tokens")]
  166|   907|             if not all(type(v) is int and v >= 0 for v in counts):
  167|   908|                 reason = "usage_not_established"
  168|   909|         amount, basis = None, None
  169|   910|         if reason is None:
  170|   911|             i, o = counts
  171|   912|             details = usage.get("input_tokens_details")
  172|   913|             if details is not None and not isinstance(details, dict):
  173|   914|                 reason = "invalid_cache_usage"
  174|   915|             else:
  175|   916|                 details = details or {}
  176|   917|                 c, w = details.get("cached_tokens"), details.get("cache_write_tokens")
  177|   918|                 if any(v is not None and (type(v) is not int or v < 0 or v > i) for v in (c, w)) or (c or 0) + (w or 0) > i:
  178|   919|                     reason = "invalid_cache_usage"
  179|   920|                 else:
  180|   921|                     if c is None or w is None:
  181|   922|                         # Missing optional cache counts: value all input at the dearer rate.
  182|   923|                         c, w = 0, i if self.rates["cache_write"] > self.rates["input"] else 0
  183|   924|                         basis = "conservative_all_input_at_dearest_rate"
  184|   925|                     else:
  185|   926|                         basis = "reported_cache_usage"
  186|   927|                     try:
  187|   928|                         amount = usage_usd(self.rates, input_tokens=i, cached=c, cache_write=w,
  188|   929|                                            output_tokens=o, multiplier=self.rates["tier_multiplier"]["default"])
  189|   930|                         if not math.isfinite(amount) or amount < 0:
  190|   931|                             reason = "valuation_not_established"
  191|   932|                     except (ArithmeticError, ValueError):
  192|   933|                         reason = "valuation_not_established"
  193|   934|         if reason:
  194|   935|             amount = None
  195|   936|             self.unknown = self.exhausted = True
  196|   937|         else:
  197|   938|             self.settled = math.fsum((self.settled, amount))
  198|   939|             self.exhausted = self.settled >= self.max_usd
  199|   940|         return {"rid": ticket["rid"], "status": "unknown" if reason else "valued",
  200|   941|                 "cost_proxy_usd": amount, "valuation_basis": basis, "reason": reason,
  201|   942|                 "session_cost_proxy_usd": self.settled, "limit_reached": self.exhausted,
  202|   943|                 "measurement": SUBSCRIPTION_PROXY_BASIS, "valuation_tier": "default",
  203|   944|                 "model": self.rates["model"], "rates_sha256": hashlib.sha256(_canonical(self.rates)).hexdigest(),
  204|   945|                 "billing": "unknown", "quota": "unknown", "limitation": SUBSCRIPTION_PROXY_LIMITATION}

  206|   947| 
  207|   948| class UsdBudget:
  208|   949|     """A session dollar limit, enforced per request. Callers hold the proxy lock.
  209|   950| 
  210|   951|     reserve() sets aside the request's worst-case cost or refuses; settle()
  211|   952|     replaces it with the charge from the provider's final usage. Reservations
  212|   953|     use the requested tier's multiplier (the dearer of it and default with
  213|   954|     strict_tier). A response served at another tier is charged at that tier,
  214|   955|     and later reservations use at least the dearer of it and default; a
  215|   956|     response reporting no tier is charged at the dearest multiplier, which
  216|   957|     then becomes that floor. After the first refusal every later request is
  217|   958|     refused.
  218|   959|     """
  219|   960| 
  220|   961|     def __init__(self, *, max_usd: float, rates: dict, tier: str | None, max_output_tokens: int,
  221|   962|                  spent_before_usd: float = 0.0, prefix: dict | None = None,
  222|   963|                  multiplier_floor: float = 0.0, strict_tier: bool = False):
  223|   964|         if (tier or "default") not in rates["tier_multiplier"]:
  224|   965|             raise ContainerError("usage", f"a dollar limit needs a priced tier, not {tier!r}")
  225|   966|         self.max_usd, self.rates, self.tier = max_usd, rates, tier or "default"
  226|   967|         self.max_output_tokens, self.strict_tier = max_output_tokens, strict_tier
  227|   968|         self.settled, self.outstanding = spent_before_usd, 0.0
  228|   969|         self.prefix, self.floor = prefix, multiplier_floor
  229|   970|         self.exhausted, self.seq = False, 0
  230|   971| 
  231|   972|     def multiplier(self) -> float:
  232|   973|         table = self.rates["tier_multiplier"]
  233|   974|         m = max(table[self.tier], table["default"]) if self.strict_tier else table[self.tier]
  234|   975|         return max(m, self.floor)
  235|   976| 
  236|   977|     def worst_case(self, bound: int) -> float:
  237|   978|         return reserve_usd(self.rates, input_bound=bound, max_output_tokens=self.max_output_tokens,
  238|   979|                            multiplier=self.multiplier())
  239|   980| 
  240|   981|     def reserve(self, basis: dict):

  276|  1017|         self.settled += charged

  278|  1019|                 "charged_usd": round(charged, 6), "charge_basis": how,
  279|  1020|                 "session_spent_usd": round(self.settled + self.outstanding, 6), "multiplier_floor": self.floor}

  282|  1023| class AnthropicUsdBudget(UsdBudget):
  283|  1024|     """UsdBudget for Anthropic Messages, with the usage ledger's rates (native_usd).
  284|  1025| 
  285|  1026|     No tiers: the proxy refuses service_tier and speed, so a request runs at
  286|  1027|     standard rates; a response reporting another tier keeps its reservation.
  287|  1028|     Worst case: every input token at the dearest of the input and both
  288|  1029|     cache-write rates, plus the output cap (thinking counts as output). Cache
  289|  1030|     writes are charged by lifetime; writes the provider did not split by
  290|  1031|     lifetime are charged at the dearer rate. No long-context rule is applied,
  291|  1032|     as in the ledger.
  292|  1033|     """
  293|  1034| 
  294|  1035|     def multiplier(self) -> float:
  295|  1036|         return 1.0
  296|  1037| 
  297|  1038|     def worst_case(self, bound: int) -> float:
  298|  1039|         r = self.rates
  299|  1040|         dearest = max(r["input"], r["cache_write_5m"], r["cache_write_1h"])
  300|  1041|         return (bound * dearest + self.max_output_tokens * r["output"]) / 1e6
  301|  1042| 
  302|  1043|     def settle(self, ticket: dict, *, http_status, response: dict) -> dict:
  303|  1044|         self.outstanding -= ticket["reserved_usd"]
  304|  1045|         u, r = response.get("usage") or {}, self.rates
  305|  1046|         i, cr, cw, o = (u.get(k) for k in ("input_tokens", "cache_read_input_tokens",
  306|  1047|                                              "cache_creation_input_tokens", "output_tokens"))
  307|  1048|         w5, w1 = u.get("ephemeral_5m_input_tokens") or 0, u.get("ephemeral_1h_input_tokens") or 0
  308|  1049|         total = None
  309|  1050|         if http_status is not None and http_status != 200:
  310|  1051|             charged, how = 0.0, "error_status"  # assumed unbilled, not verified
  311|  1052|         elif http_status is not None \
  312|  1053|                 and all(isinstance(x, int) and not isinstance(x, bool) and x >= 0 for x in (i, cr, cw, o, w5, w1)) \
  313|  1054|                 and w5 + w1 <= cw and u.get("service_tier") in (None, "standard"):
  314|  1055|             # http_status None: the stream ended before message_stop, so the
  315|  1056|             # usage seen so far (message_start) understates output; keep the reservation.
  316|  1057|             charged = (i * r["input"] + cr * r["cache_read"] + w5 * r["cache_write_5m"] + w1 * r["cache_write_1h"]
  317|  1058|                        + (cw - w5 - w1) * max(r["cache_write_5m"], r["cache_write_1h"]) + o * r["output"]) / 1e6
  318|  1059|             how, total = "usage", i + cr + cw
  319|  1060|             basis = ticket["basis"]
  320|  1061|             self.prefix = {"items": basis["items"], "chain_sha256": basis["chains"][-1],
  321|  1062|                            "rest_sha256": basis["rest_sha256"], "input_tokens": total}
  322|  1063|         else:
  323|  1064|             charged, how = ticket["reserved_usd"], "reservation"
  324|  1065|         self.settled += charged
  325|  1066|         out = {"rid": ticket["rid"], "reserved_usd": round(ticket["reserved_usd"], 6),
  326|  1067|                "charged_usd": round(charged, 6), "charge_basis": how,
  327|  1068|                "session_spent_usd": round(self.settled + self.outstanding, 6), "multiplier_floor": self.floor}
  328|  1069|         if total is not None:
  329|  1070|             out["input_total"] = total
  330|  1071|         return out

===== BEGIN FILE doctrine/PHILOSOPHY.md | sha256 299b0ad8192f3cc442f6491d2908f421f22333024ffb1423cae6856b69c3a307 | 34973 bytes | 885 lines =====
    1| # Work Engine Philosophy
    2| 
    3| This document explains the reasoning behind Work Engine's design principles.
    4| 
    5| It is intentionally **non-normative**.
    6| 
    7| `DESIGN.md` defines the product's structural contracts and governing design rules. This file explains why those rules exist, what failure modes motivated them, and how we think about model-centered workflows. New implementation requirements should not be inferred from this document unless they are promoted into `DESIGN.md` as explicit product structure.
    8| 
    9| ---
   10| 
   11| ## 1. Model judgment is the default problem-solving mechanism
   12| 
   13| Work Engine is built around a simple observation:
   14| 
   15| > A capable model usually performs better when it is given an objective, relevant evidence, available capabilities, and real constraints than when it is given a detailed procedure for solving the problem.
   16| 
   17| Procedures are attractive because they feel safe and deterministic. They can also be harmful.
   18| 
   19| A procedural rule creates a high-confidence action path. Once the current situation resembles the rule's trigger, following the rule becomes an inexpensive way for the model to satisfy the instruction. That can suppress contextual alternatives even when those alternatives would be better.
   20| 
   21| The failure mode is not merely rigidity. Enough procedural rules can make the model appear less intelligent:
   22| 
   23| ```text
   24| observe situation
   25| → match procedure
   26| → execute known path
   27| ```
   28| 
   29| replaces:
   30| 
   31| ```text
   32| observe situation
   33| → build a model of the problem
   34| → understand consequences
   35| → compare possible actions
   36| → choose
   37| ```
   38| 
   39| The purpose of Work Engine is not to remove structure. It is to place structure only where structure is load-bearing.
   40| 
   41| ---
   42| 
   43| ## 2. Structure and judgment are different things
   44| 
   45| We distinguish several kinds of model-facing information:
   46| 
   47| ```text
   48| invariant structure   → what must remain true
   49| variant structure     → what currently exists and why
   50| state / evidence      → what is true in this run
   51| objective/consequence → what outcome matters and what failure means
   52| judgment              → what to do
   53| ```
   54| 
   55| This separation is central.
   56| 
   57| The model should not be asked to infer product contracts that the product already knows. At the same time, the product should not convert today's preferred method into tomorrow's mandatory procedure.
   58| 
   59| ---
   60| 
   61| ## 3. Invariant structure as the hard frame
   62| 
   63| The canonical taxonomy for Work Engine is the five-way split introduced above:
   64| 
   65| ```text
   66| invariant structure
   67| variant structure
   68| state / evidence
   69| objective / consequence
   70| judgment
   71| ```
   72| 
   73| The older **hard map / fuzzy map** language is only a metaphor for one relationship inside that taxonomy. It is not a second ontology.
   74| 
   75| The **hard frame** corresponds specifically to **invariant structure**: the product facts that must not drift with interpretation.
   76| 
   77| Examples include:
   78| 
   79| - ownership;
   80| - authority;
   81| - read/write boundaries;
   82| - required interface shapes;
   83| - security constraints;
   84| - provenance obligations;
   85| - human-approval boundaries;
   86| - other properties whose violation makes the product operate incorrectly.
   87| 
   88| These are stable reference points.
   89| 
   90| > **Pin the coordinate system, not the path.**
   91| 
   92| The purpose of invariant structure is not simply restriction. It removes foundational questions the model should not have to repeatedly infer.
   93| 
   94| Without structural anchors, a model may have to reason about questions such as:
   95| 
   96| - Who owns this?
   97| - May this component mutate that?
   98| - Is this artifact authoritative?
   99| - What shape does the downstream consumer require?
  100| - Does this transition require provenance?
  101| - Is this review meant to be independent?
  102| 
  103| If the product already knows the invariant answers, leaving those dimensions open merely increases the reasoning problem.
  104| 
  105| Invariant structure removes invalid degrees of freedom.
  106| 
  107| > **Remove invalid degrees of freedom while preserving every legitimate degree of freedom.**
  108| 
  109| That is the intended balance.
  110| 
  111| ---
  112| 
  113| ## 4. The fuzzy operational map
  114| 
  115| The **fuzzy map** is a metaphor for the mutable operational world the model is working inside. It includes the current **variant structure**, the current **state/evidence**, and the model's revisable interpretation of what those facts imply.
  116| 
  117| "Fuzzy" does not mean vague, low-confidence, or poorly understood.
  118| 
  119| When the model zooms into the map, it should ideally see a machine it understands: known controls, known capabilities, known state, prior context, and known consequences. What makes the map fuzzy is that its configuration and state can change.
  120| 
  121| Variant structure is therefore a major part of the fuzzy map:
  122| 
  123| - the current machinery;
  124| - the controls the model can manipulate;
  125| - the capabilities available;
  126| - agent and reviewer lifetimes;
  127| - provider arrangements;
  128| - component topology;
  129| - current configuration.
  130| 
  131| State/evidence describes where that machinery and its environment are now.
  132| 
  133| Judgment determines which available manipulation is appropriate.
  134| 
  135| A useful relationship is:
  136| 
  137| ```text
  138| INVARIANT STRUCTURE
  139| fixed product frame
  140|         │
  141|         ▼
  142| FUZZY OPERATIONAL MAP
  143| variant machinery + current state + evidence
  144|         │
  145|         ▼
  146| JUDGMENT
  147| choose how to manipulate the environment
  148|         │
  149|         ▼
  150| NEW OPERATIONAL STATE
  151| ```
  152| 
  153| The hard map does not move during normal product operation. The fuzzy map does.
  154| 
  155| A surprising new fact should usually change the fuzzy operational map without forcing the product's invariant structure to be reconstructed.
  156| 
  157| The fixed frame keeps foundational product relationships stable while the operational map changes.
  158| 
  159| The model should not need to reconstruct the machine itself every time it acts. It should be able to understand the machinery, inspect its current state, and decide what state transition makes sense now.
  160| 
  161| ---
  162| 
  163| ## 5. Why this resembles human structure
  164| 
  165| Human reasoning also occurs inside a mixture of stable and changing structure.
  166| 
  167| A person does not normally reconsider every fundamental relationship or environmental regularity before choosing what to do next. Physical reality, social roles, relationships, responsibilities, institutions, and other durable structures constrain the range of plausible interpretation and action.
  168| 
  169| A person may be a parent, spouse, employee, student, tenant, citizen, or caregiver. Those roles do not dictate every action. They constrain and organize the space in which action is chosen.
  170| 
  171| Likewise, ordinary environmental regularities provide a stable frame. People do not need to reconstruct their whole model of the world every morning before acting.
  172| 
  173| Work Engine deliberately provides its models with an analogous kind of structural certainty.
  174| 
  175| The analogy is not that a model reasons exactly like a human. The useful point is architectural: **stable structure reduces the number of dimensions that must remain open at once.**
  176| 
  177| That can make reasoning more efficient and more coherent.
  178| 
  179| ---
  180| 
  181| ## 6. Good constraints and bad constraints both reduce semantic space
  182| 
  183| All strong instructions constrain the model's possible continuations.
  184| 
  185| The important question is **what they remove**.
  186| 
  187| A good structural invariant removes invalid worlds:
  188| 
  189| ```text
  190| reviewer mutates implementation
  191| → independence claim becomes false
  192| → that state is invalid
  193| ```
  194| 
  195| A bad procedural rule removes legitimate solutions:
  196| 
  197| ```text
  198| always use capability A before capability B
  199| → alternatives disappear
  200| even when B is clearly better for this claim
  201| ```
  202| 
  203| Both reduce the model's action space.
  204| 
  205| Only one reduces the right part of it.
  206| 
  207| This distinction explains why Work Engine is not anti-command or anti-constraint. Correctly chosen constraints reduce complexity. Incorrectly chosen procedural constraints reduce capability.
  208| 
  209| A useful test is route-invariance: if a resulting state would be valid when reached by one legitimate route but invalid only because another route was not followed, the disputed requirement is probably procedural rather than invariant. Likewise, consequences should normally describe the property that must be established or preserved, not the activity currently believed to establish it. Otherwise procedure can re-enter the system disguised as either an invariant or a consequence.
  210| 
  211| ---
  212| 
  213| ## 7. Boundary trimming, interior enrichment, and path concentration
  214| 
  215| There are three different ways model-facing structure can change the effective problem space.
  216| 
  217| ### Invariant structure trims the boundary
  218| 
  219| Invariant structure removes worlds the product considers invalid.
  220| 
  221| It does not choose among the remaining valid solutions. It simply says that some states, transitions, or outcomes are outside the product contract.
  222| 
  223| This is productive reduction of the search space because the removed possibilities were never legitimate solutions.
  224| 
  225| ### Variant structure enriches the interior
  226| 
  227| Variant structure does not primarily remove possibilities. It describes the machinery available inside the valid space and can make additional valid outcomes reachable.
  228| 
  229| Capabilities, controls, lifetimes, provider arrangements, topology, and state transitions add usable dimensions to the model's operational environment.
  230| 
  231| This enrichment should be meaningful rather than redundant. A new capability is valuable when it adds expressive reach or makes an important distinction legible. Multiple indistinguishable controls that lead to the same result can increase search complexity without adding useful agency.
  232| 
  233| ### Procedures create behavioral path attractors
  234| 
  235| Procedures are different from both.
  236| 
  237| A procedure usually operates **inside** the already-valid space. It says, explicitly or implicitly, that one route should receive much more behavioral weight than other valid routes, creating a behavioral attractor toward that path.
  238| 
  239| That is the dangerous form of narrowing.
  240| 
  241| A useful shorthand is:
  242| 
  243| > **Trim invalid space. Enrich valid space. Avoid unnecessary path concentration inside valid space.**
  244| 
  245| The concern is not that procedures literally expose a readable probability table. The claim is behavioral and mechanistic: instruction-tuned models are trained to respond strongly to explicit directives, so a literal procedure can sharply bias the continuation toward compliance.
  246| 
  247| This should not be read as privileged introspection into model logits. It is an architectural model grounded in known instruction-following behavior and observable agent behavior, not a claim that the model can inspect its own token probabilities.
  248| 
  249| In coding-agent settings, where decoding is often configured for deterministic behavior, that bias can become especially visible.
  250| 
  251| The important failure mode is that procedural concentration may occur **before** useful exploration.
  252| 
  253| Instead of:
  254| 
  255| ```text
  256| observe
  257| → explore evidence
  258| → generate alternatives
  259| → compare consequences
  260| → choose
  261| ```
  262| 
  263| the model may effectively do:
  264| 
  265| ```text
  266| observe
  267| → match known procedure
  268| → follow procedure
  269| ```
  270| 
  271| If that happens early enough, the evidence that would have revealed a better route may never be sought or generated.
  272| 
  273| This gives a plausible explanation for a recurring Work Engine observation: adding more procedural guidance can make a capable model appear less capable even in areas where it technically still has permission to choose.
  274| 
  275| The model may not be choosing badly after considering alternatives. It may be generating fewer alternatives in the first place.
  276| 
  277| Coding is particularly sensitive to this because most coding problems are open, multimodal spaces. Many implementations can satisfy the same specification. A procedure that locks in one route therefore discards alternatives the procedure author often had no basis to exclude.
  278| 
  279| The target state is:
  280| 
  281| ```text
  282| real invariant boundary
  283|         ↓
  284| valid problem space
  285|         ↓
  286| rich, composable machinery
  287|         ↓
  288| model judgment over open valid routes
  289| ```
  290| 
  291| not:
  292| 
  293| ```text
  294| valid problem space
  295|         ↓
  296| rich machinery
  297|         ↓
  298| procedure selects one anticipated route
  299|         ↓
  300| model mostly executes that route
  301| ```
  302| 
  303| ---
  304| 
  305| 
  306| ## 8. Commands are structural anchors
  307| 
  308| Commands should represent the minimum product structure required for the system to function correctly.
  309| 
  310| They should encode things such as:
  311| 
  312| - authority;
  313| - ownership;
  314| - interface contracts;
  315| - security;
  316| - mutation boundaries;
  317| - provenance requirements;
  318| - explicit approval boundaries.
  319| 
  320| They should not encode mutable problem knowledge or preferred routes.
  321| 
  322| A command like:
  323| 
  324| > Reviewers used as independent evidence are read-only.
  325| 
  326| is structural.
  327| 
  328| A command like:
  329| 
  330| > Use Codebase Memory first and direct filesystem reads only when Codebase Memory fails.
  331| 
  332| is procedural.
  333| 
  334| The first establishes a product property. The second encodes one solution strategy.
  335| 
  336| ---
  337| 
  338| ## 9. Every command should have a causal parent
  339| 
  340| A bare command is easy to follow but poor at supporting generalization.
  341| 
  342| Compare:
  343| 
  344| > Always output `ReceiptSchema`.
  345| 
  346| with:
  347| 
  348| > The downstream consumer validates against `ReceiptSchema`. A nonconforming receipt cannot be consumed and invalidates the audit artifact. Emit a receipt conforming to `ReceiptSchema`.
  349| 
  350| The second contains both reason and command.
  351| 
  352| That matters because the reason creates **continuity of logic**.
  353| 
  354| The model can carry the causal relationship forward into later reasoning:
  355| 
  356| ```text
  357| causal reality
  358| → failure mode
  359| → invariant
  360| → minimal command
  361| → later judgment
  362| ```
  363| 
  364| The command remains firm, but it is not an unexplained island in the context.
  365| 
  366| We call this the **Logical Continuity Principle**:
  367| 
  368| > A structural command should be connected to the causal reality that makes it necessary, so the reason for the invariant remains available to later reasoning.
  369| 
  370| This is especially valuable in novel situations. The model can preserve the underlying property instead of blindly repeating a ritual whose purpose it does not understand.
  371| 
  372| ---
  373| 
  374| ## 10. Logical discontinuity and inferential debt
  375| 
  376| Section 7 described how procedural pressure can concentrate traversal before alternatives are explored. A related but distinct failure occurs when a generated assertion is not supported by a reasoning path that later analysis can reconstruct or endorse.
  377| 
  378| **Logical discontinuity** occurs when an assertion enters the interaction but later analysis, using the surrounding evidence and logic, would reject or materially revise it.
  379| 
  380| The claim is behavioral rather than introspective. The relevant observation is not that the model secretly held another internal belief. It is simply that the earlier output does not align with what the model produces when that output is itself subjected to analysis.
  381| 
  382| Once emitted, however, the assertion becomes part of the transcript and therefore part of the evidence presented to later reasoning. If its causal support is missing or stale, subsequent reasoning may continue accommodating, defending, reinterpreting, or reasoning around it.
  383| 
  384| We call the resulting burden **inferential debt**:
  385| 
  386| > **Inferential debt is the downstream reasoning cost created when an unsupported or stale assertion remains active in context.**
  387| 
  388| The original unsupported assertion is the principal. The repeated work required to reconcile later reasoning around it is the interest. That cost can include distorted interpretation of new evidence, suppression of alternatives that conflict with the prior assertion, unnecessary rationalization, and new conclusions that inherit the stale premise.
  389| 
  390| The propagation mechanism does not depend on procedures specifically. Any unsupported self-generated conclusion may create downstream debt once it becomes part of the interaction history.
  391| 
  392| The narrower Work Engine hypothesis is that strong procedural or directive pressure can be a particularly reliable producer of the initial discontinuity. The same match-and-execute shortcut discussed in Section 7 can produce content that bypasses broader evaluation, not only alternative generation.
  393| 
  394| ### Logical continuity also enables revisability
  395| 
  396| Section 9 introduced the Logical Continuity Principle primarily as a generalization aid. It has a second function: **revisability**.
  397| 
  398| A command connected to its causal parent gives later reasoning something it can re-evaluate. If the causal premise changes, the model can identify what downstream conclusion has become stale rather than carrying an unexplained authoritative statement forward.
  399| 
  400| A bare command is therefore dangerous not merely because it is restrictive. **It creates an authoritative node without an explicit dependency edge.**
  401| 
  402| Logical continuity serves two related purposes:
  403| 
  404| - **Generalization** — preserve the underlying property when circumstances change.
  405| - **Revisability** — identify which conclusions cease to hold when their causal support changes.
  406| 
  407| This does not require preserving a full reasoning transcript or an elaborate dependency graph. The system needs only enough durable consequence to support later judgment.
  408| 
  409| ### Route revision retires stale consequences
  410| 
  411| Route revision is the corresponding repair mechanism when continuity breaks or evidence falsifies a route.
  412| 
  413| When an attempted route fails, retain the smallest durable fact needed to prevent unnecessary reconsideration: **what was tried, what premise justified trying it, and what observation invalidated it.**
  414| 
  415| For example:
  416| 
  417| ```text
  418| X was attempted
  419| because Y made it plausible
  420| observation Z invalidated that route
  421| ```
  422| 
  423| The useful durable consequence is not the full history of how X was chosen. It is that **Y no longer supports X under the conditions established by Z**. This is enough to eliminate a previously tested option from the live route space under the same relevant conditions without pretending to preserve a full causal graph.
  424| 
  425| That failed attempt becomes evidence about the route rather than inert history. Later reasoning can remove that option under the same relevant conditions without reconstructing the entire investigation.
  426| 
  427| Route revision therefore **retires identified inferential debt**. When evidence invalidates a premise or route, the resulting stale conclusion should be retired explicitly rather than silently reconciled with later evidence. It cannot guarantee discovery of every downstream conclusion that inherited the stale premise, so the claim is deliberately narrower: debt that has been identified should not remain authoritative merely because it is already in the transcript. Observations and decisions that remain valid should survive; only the consequences made stale by the new evidence should be revised.
  428| 
  429| ---
  430| 
  431| ## 11. Variant structure: describe the machine, not how to use it
  432| 
  433| Not all structure is invariant.
  434| 
  435| The product also has **variant structure**: machinery that exists today but may evolve.
  436| 
  437| Examples include:
  438| 
  439| - available capabilities;
  440| - agent topology;
  441| - persistence mechanisms;
  442| - provider arrangements;
  443| - current component boundaries;
  444| - retrieval infrastructure;
  445| - review infrastructure;
  446| - telemetry mechanisms;
  447| - repository adapters.
  448| 
  449| Variant structure should be presented as:
  450| 
  451| - what exists;
  452| - what it affords;
  453| - why it exists;
  454| - what tradeoffs shaped it;
  455| - what consequences follow from its current design.
  456| 
  457| It should not automatically become a procedure.
  458| 
  459| For example:
  460| 
  461| > Codebase Memory is optimized for structural repository understanding. Direct filesystem tools provide source-level observation and can reach material outside the index.
  462| 
  463| This teaches the model what the machinery is good at.
  464| 
  465| It does not say:
  466| 
  467| > Always use Codebase Memory first.
  468| 
  469| The distinction is important.
  470| 
  471| Variant structure should enrich the model's understanding of the environment, not dictate interaction with that environment.
  472| 
  473| A useful pattern is:
  474| 
  475| ```text
  476| current machinery
  477| → rationale
  478| → affordances and limitations
  479| → model judgment
  480| ```
  481| 
  482| There is deliberately no mandatory action at the end unless an invariant requires one.
  483| 
  484| ---
  485| 
  486| ## 12. Capability affordances instead of routing tables
  487| 
  488| A capability description should tell the model what a tool can establish and what kinds of evidence it exposes.
  489| 
  490| The model then chooses among available capabilities according to the current claim.
  491| 
  492| This has practical benefits.
  493| 
  494| A reviewer with both indexed repository knowledge and direct read-only filesystem access can move between them without losing context:
  495| 
  496| ```text
  497| structural question
  498| → indexed evidence
  499| 
  500| exact source claim
  501| → direct source observation
  502| 
  503| new relationship question
  504| → indexed evidence again
  505| ```
  506| 
  507| There is no need to model this as a rigid transition between modes.
  508| 
  509| However, actual fallback remains auditable.
  510| 
  511| These are different events:
  512| 
  513| **Capability selection**
  514| 
  515| The model freely chooses the evidence source best suited to the claim.
  516| 
  517| **Evidence fallback**
  518| 
  519| An earlier evidence route proves unavailable, ambiguous, insufficient, or failed, and another route is used because of that failure.
  520| 
  521| **Provider/configuration fallback**
  522| 
  523| The configured provider itself must change.
  524| 
  525| Only the latter two are fallback events. Their required provenance remains a product contract.
  526| 
  527| Flexibility must not weaken auditability.
  528| 
  529| ---
  530| 
  531| ## 13. Variant structure as a manipulable state machine
  532| 
  533| Variant structure is not merely a description of components. It is the model's **manipulable environment**.
  534| 
  535| A useful mental model is a state machine with controls.
  536| 
  537| The product can either prescribe a path:
  538| 
  539| > To accomplish X, turn knob Y and press button Z.
  540| 
  541| or describe the machine:
  542| 
  543| > Knob Y controls pressure. Button Z commits the current configuration. Indicator Q reports stability.
  544| 
  545| The second form preserves agency.
  546| 
  547| The model understands what each control does and can compose controls according to the current state and objective. The combinations of those controls become the model's way of manipulating environmental state.
  548| 
  549| This makes interface design an action-space design problem.
  550| 
  551| ### Insufficient agency
  552| 
  553| If the model is not given enough controls, some legitimate outcomes are unreachable.
  554| 
  555| The model may correctly understand what should happen but have no available sequence of actions capable of making it happen.
  556| 
  557| That is a product failure even if the model's reasoning is correct.
  558| 
  559| ### Excessive agency
  560| 
  561| If the model is given too many overlapping, redundant, or poorly distinguished controls, the number of possible action combinations grows without adding equivalent value.
  562| 
  563| The model must then spend reasoning effort choosing among multiple paths that lead to the same state or interpreting distinctions that do not matter.
  564| 
  565| That enlarges the search space unnecessarily.
  566| 
  567| ### The intended balance
  568| 
  569| The goal is not maximum freedom.
  570| 
  571| The goal is:
  572| 
  573| > **Provide enough composable machinery to make intended outcomes reachable, but do not expose redundant or irrelevant degrees of freedom that only enlarge the model's search space.**
  574| 
  575| This is the canonical action-space principle from `DESIGN.md`; the discussion below expands its reasoning rather than redefining it.
  576| 
  577| The machinery should be compositionally rich enough that the model can construct novel solutions from a small set of meaningful primitives, without requiring one dedicated control for every anticipated situation.
  578| 
  579| This is similar to a good instruction set: a small number of coherent primitives can generate sophisticated behavior when their semantics are clear and their combinations are expressive.
  580| 
  581| For example, reviewer recovery need not require a dedicated `recover_from_stale_reviewer_context()` procedure if the environment already exposes meaningful primitives such as:
  582| 
  583| - inspect reviewer state;
  584| - continue reviewer;
  585| - checkpoint context;
  586| - spawn a new reviewer;
  587| - supply context;
  588| - terminate reviewer.
  589| 
  590| The model can compose those controls according to the actual situation.
  591| 
  592| ### Interface complexity is reasoning complexity
  593| 
  594| Every control exposed to the model introduces another possible state transition or combination of transitions.
  595| 
  596| That means interface design directly shapes the model's reasoning space.
  597| 
  598| A useful test for a new capability is:
  599| 
  600| > Does this control make a legitimate state transition reachable that the model could not already express cleanly?
  601| 
  602| And the inverse:
  603| 
  604| > Does this control merely add another path to an already-reachable state without adding useful meaning?
  605| 
  606| Variant structure exists to shape that action space deliberately.
  607| 
  608| Invariant structure removes invalid states and invalid transitions.
  609| 
  610| Variant structure exposes the valid machinery.
  611| 
  612| State/evidence tells the model where it is.
  613| 
  614| Objectives and consequences tell it what matters.
  615| 
  616| Judgment chooses the transition.
  617| 
  618| ```text
  619| current state
  620| + available transitions
  621| + invariant constraints
  622| + objective / consequences
  623| + evidence
  624| ────────────────────────
  625| judgment
  626|     ↓
  627| chosen manipulation
  628|     ↓
  629| new state
  630| ```
  631| 
  632| This is the operational core of model-centered control.
  633| 
  634| ---
  635| 
  636| ## 14. Context has value and lifetime
  637| 
  638| > **Preserve the consequence of reasoning, not necessarily the reasoning transcript.**
  639| 
  640| Model context is not merely temporary storage. It contains accumulated interpretation.
  641| 
  642| Discarding useful context forces the system to reconstruct:
  643| 
  644| - architectural understanding;
  645| - prior findings;
  646| - accepted invariants;
  647| - unresolved questions;
  648| - evidence already evaluated;
  649| - reasoning about why previous findings mattered.
  650| 
  651| This can be extremely expensive.
  652| 
  653| For that reason, context lifetime should be chosen according to the value of retained reasoning.
  654| 
  655| A reviewer is a good example.
  656| 
  657| A useful lifecycle is:
  658| 
  659| ```text
  660| fresh independent reviewer
  661| → initial review
  662| → findings
  663| → builder remediation
  664| → same reviewer evaluates the delta
  665| → repeat while context remains useful
  666| → discard
  667| ```
  668| 
  669| Freshness matters at entry because independence matters.
  670| 
  671| Persistence matters during remediation because the reviewer already understands the problem.
  672| 
  673| The choice to retain or reset that context should itself be model judgment unless a product contract imposes a boundary.
  674| 
  675| This follows the same philosophy: preserve useful structure, preserve useful knowledge, and avoid procedural lifecycle rules that destroy value without a causal reason.
  676| 
  677| ---
  678| 
  679| ## 15. High-level procedures can still be useful
  680| 
  681| Work Engine does not require the absence of procedures.
  682| 
  683| A high-level procedure can encode accumulated experience and provide a good default path.
  684| 
  685| For example, a high-assurance change may commonly benefit from:
  686| 
  687| ```text
  688| explore
  689| → choose
  690| → state the architectural claim
  691| → falsify
  692| → implement
  693| → prove
  694| ```
  695| 
  696| The important distinction is that such a route is a **hypothesis about a useful process**, not an invariant product contract.
  697| 
  698| The model may revise it when current evidence makes another route better, provided all true invariants and required consequences remain satisfied.
  699| 
  700| Procedures should therefore have escape by judgment, not exhaustive exception tables.
  701| 
  702| An exception table tries to enumerate semantic space.
  703| 
  704| Judgment allows the model to interpret semantic space.
  705| 
  706| ---
  707| 
  708| ## 16. Why exhaustive rules are dangerous
  709| 
  710| A procedural author can rarely anticipate the complete set of future situations.
  711| 
  712| Rules such as:
  713| 
  714| > If X, do Y.
  715| 
  716| are especially dangerous when X belongs to an open semantic domain.
  717| 
  718| As rules accumulate, the product may accidentally flatten a rich problem space into the designer's finite taxonomy of known cases.
  719| 
  720| The model then has a cheap path:
  721| 
  722| ```text
  723| classify case
  724| → find matching rule
  725| → execute
  726| ```
  727| 
  728| This is attractive computationally and can suppress deeper reasoning even where judgment was intended.
  729| 
  730| The preferred alternative is to describe:
  731| 
  732| - the real constraint;
  733| - the consequence of violating it;
  734| - the machinery available;
  735| - the evidence currently known;
  736| - the objective.
  737| 
  738| Then allow the model to reason.
  739| 
  740| ---
  741| 
  742| ## 17. The role of the design document
  743| 
  744| The design philosophy should not itself become workflow bloat.
  745| 
  746| That is why this file is separate from `DESIGN.md`.
  747| 
  748| `DESIGN.md` should contain the compressed product doctrine:
  749| 
  750| - the invariant structure;
  751| - the first-class categories;
  752| - the minimum design tests needed to keep new features aligned.
  753| 
  754| This file may contain:
  755| 
  756| - metaphors;
  757| - examples;
  758| - historical reasoning;
  759| - failure modes;
  760| - competing formulations;
  761| - explanations of why the design evolved.
  762| 
  763| The distinction is deliberate:
  764| 
  765| > **Conversation and philosophy may be expansive. Product doctrine should be compressed.**
  766| 
  767| Runtime instructions form a narrower projection again. An agent should normally receive only the invariant structure, consequences, authority boundaries, and capability affordances relevant to its role and current machinery. Those instructions should remain traceable to the owning design rather than becoming an independent policy layer that can silently drift into new procedure.
  768| 
  769| A future author should be able to understand the "why" here without forcing every agent to ingest the entire reasoning history during ordinary execution. The same context-economy principle applies across the documentation stack: preserve the consequence and lineage needed for correct judgment, not every layer of explanatory history in every runtime context.
  770| 
  771| ---
  772| 
  773| ## 18. Production-path claims, consumption, and establishment
  774| 
  775| The route-invariance test needs one careful qualification. Most required
  776| properties concern the resulting artifact or consequence, but some acceptance
  777| claims quantify over how that result was produced. Independence,
  778| preregistration, evidence custody, treatment exposure, fallback provenance, and
  779| effect duplication can all make production history relevant.
  780| 
  781| That does not make every preference about production history structural. A
  782| route-sensitive claim earns contractual force only when it is already required
  783| by an owner outside the local mechanism seeking to constrain the route, and a
  784| violating route causes a failure that can be named without saying merely that
  785| the route rule was broken. Otherwise any preferred procedure could authorize
  786| itself by inventing a claim whose only consumer is the procedure.
  787| 
  788| ### Claim locus and the recovery frontier are different questions
  789| 
  790| One question is where the fact asserted by the claim exists.
  791| 
  792| A **path-constitutive claim** is about the execution itself. Whether a reviewer
  793| was exposed to builder reasoning, whether a specification author saw outcomes,
  794| or whether evidence remained under required custody cannot be established from
  795| the terminal artifact alone. The relevant evidence must come from the runtime,
  796| harness, custody system, or another observer of the production path.
  797| 
  798| A **world-history claim** is about what happened outside the actor. Whether an
  799| external effect occurred twice is not necessarily visible in a terminal
  800| snapshot, but an independently owned effect ledger may establish it. The
  801| acting role's own log is still an attributable record of what the role reports;
  802| it is not independent observation of the world merely because it has ledger
  803| shape.
  804| 
  805| The second question is where a violation becomes consequential. The important
  806| boundary is consumption, not elapsed time in isolation. A path must be
  807| constrained when a violating fact could enter the state covered by the claim
  808| before available evidence allows it to be fenced, discarded, repaired, or
  809| replaced. Faster detection matters because it can move that frontier, not
  810| because latency is an independent design value.
  811| 
  812| Consumption is relative to the exact claim. A contaminated reviewer episode
  813| cannot be made uncontaminated, but a new isolated reviewer may still establish
  814| a new claim if the owning acceptance condition permits replacement and the
  815| discarded episode's output has not entered the state covered by the original
  816| claim. If the output has already crossed that boundary, replacement may no
  817| longer restore the broader claim. Substituting a narrower claim changes what
  818| the consumer required and therefore belongs to that consumer, not to the actor
  819| whose available evidence supports only the weaker statement.
  820| 
  821| ### Constraint, recording, and observation answer different questions
  822| 
  823| Constraint preserves a required property by preventing an invalid fact from
  824| occurring or propagating. Recording provides evidence of what the actor says it
  825| did. Independent observation provides evidence of what occurred from a source
  826| outside the actor's authority in the respect the claim depends on. These are
  827| composable mechanisms rather than steps or a ladder. A single claim may need
  828| more than one.
  829| 
  830| For example, independent review may use capability constraints to prevent
  831| mutation and realization evidence to attest which capabilities were actually
  832| granted. Preregistration may constrain outcome visibility and preserve an
  833| independently attributable frozen specification. An at-most-once effect may use
  834| fencing or idempotency to prevent duplication and an independently owned effect
  835| ledger to establish world history. Fallback provenance may need only an
  836| actor-attributed route record when that is all its consumer requires.
  837| 
  838| The evidence form follows the acceptance claim. Self-recording is sufficient
  839| when attributable actor conduct is the required fact. It is insufficient when
  840| the consumer requires evidence independent of the actor or when the actor
  841| cannot know the world history being asserted. Conversely, independent
  842| observation does not repair a violation that has already crossed an
  843| irreversible consumption boundary; it may establish only that the required
  844| claim is false.
  845| 
  846| ### Truth, establishment, and authority must remain separate
  847| 
  848| A constraint can preserve a property without producing admissible evidence
  849| that it held. A realization can therefore leave a claim unestablished even
  850| when the protected property may in fact be true. Treating missing attestation
  851| as truth repeats the same epistemic error as inferring zero from an unavailable
  852| measurement.
  853| 
  854| An unestablished claim is not an invitation for the actor to weaken the claim,
  855| declare success, or manufacture a global halt. It means the acceptance
  856| condition depending on that claim has not been met. The owner of that condition
  857| retains the authority to require another realization, permit a replacement
  858| subject, change or waive the condition, proceed under explicitly different
  859| acceptance, or allow the objective to fail truthfully. A requirement that no
  860| permitted realization could establish is instead a contract-formation defect;
  861| it should be exposed before a particular realization is treated as executable.
  862| 
  863| This reasoning is intentionally more expansive than the corresponding rule in
  864| `DESIGN.md`. Runtime projections need the applicable claim, boundary, evidence
  865| and authority consequences, not a general procedure for classifying every
  866| case.
  867| 
  868| ---
  869| 
  870| ## 19. A compact statement of the philosophy
  871| 
  872| Work Engine aims to give models:
  873| 
  874| - a small, stable set of structural anchors;
  875| - clear causal explanations for those anchors;
  876| - an accurate description of the machinery currently available;
  877| - the relevant state and evidence;
  878| - explicit objectives and consequences;
  879| - freedom to choose the method.
  880| 
  881| The intended result is neither unrestricted agency nor procedural automation.
  882| 
  883| It is a model-centered system in which:
  884| 
  885| > **Structure defines the valid world. Knowledge describes the current world. Judgment chooses the path through it.**
===== END FILE doctrine/PHILOSOPHY.md =====

    1| #!/usr/bin/env python3
    2| """Standalone validator for the Pi attempt-accounting corpus (`pi-accounting-corpus/1`, contract 1.6-s1, revision 7).
    3| 
    4| It is a development tool, not a reducer, and shares no code with any future implementation. It never derives an
    5| expected outcome from inputs except where a check is stated as arithmetic (price derivations from the rate files),
    6| as a contract rule over raw producer rows (forwarding, execution, tier sets, lifecycle facts, obligation intervals,
    7| closure state), or as internal consistency (expected fields that the contract ties together). Standard library only,
    8| deterministic, offline.
    9| 
   10|   validate_corpus.py check      <corpus_dir> <rates_dir>                  validate the corpus itself
   11|   validate_corpus.py results    <corpus_dir> <results.json> [--scope s2a] grade an implementation's result set
   12|   validate_corpus.py selftest   <corpus_dir> <rates_dir>                  prove defective result sets fail, each for its reason
   13|   validate_corpus.py revisions  <corpus_dir> <previous_corpus_dir>        every changed field is a logged revision-6 entry
   14|   validate_corpus.py s2a-subset <corpus_dir>                              print the S2a grading subset (selection rule)
   15| 
   16| Exit status 0 = pass, 1 = failures (listed), 2 = usage."""

   26| DECISIONS = {'absent', 'valid', 'rejected', 'conflicting', 'not_applicable'}
   27| BASES = {'usage', 'reservation', 'upstream_error', 'error_status'}
   28| REJECT = {'ReservationConflict', 'NoReservation', 'Order', 'AttemptNumber', 'AmountType', 'ReservedMismatch', 'BasisMissing',
   29|           'BasisUnknown', 'KeepAll', 'ErrorCharge', 'StatusType', 'UsageUnpriceable', 'ModelMismatch', 'Undercharge', 'ToleranceRange'}
   30| OBLIGATION = {('closed', 'settlement_amount'), ('closed', 'reservation_confirmed'), ('closed', 'reservation_bound'),
   31|               ('outstanding', 'reservation_bound'), ('none', 'none')}
   32| BLOCKING = {'SettlementRejected', 'SettlementConflict', 'ReservationConflict', 'SettlementWithoutReservation',
   33|             'IdentityContradiction', 'LaneContradiction', 'UnadmittedMeasurement', 'UnaccountedAttempt', 'UnresolvedExposure',
   34|             'UnreadableEvidence', 'InventoryIncomplete', 'DuplicateResponseIdentity', 'ArithmeticFailure', 'RateTableUnusable',
   35|             'AttributionContradiction', 'ReopenedWithoutAdmission', 'CommitmentInvalid', 'MalformedEvidence', 'UnadmittedExecution',
   36|             'UnenforcedCommitment', 'GateContradicted'}
   37| OBJ_REASONS = BLOCKING | {'OwnershipUncertain', 'HistoricalUnadmitted', 'PlacementUnplaceable', 'PlacementStraddles', 'ClosureNotEstablished'}
   38| DIAGNOSTICS = {'RepeatedIdenticalRow', 'InconsistentCacheSplit', 'NonBillingUnverified', 'IdentityAbsent', 'ProducerLogCopy',
   39|                'ErrorStatusSettlement', 'ReadFailurePaired', 'PartialUsage', 'UnfinishedAdmission', 'UncertainCodexCall'}
   40| LOW_BASES = {'none', 'usage_valuation'}
   41| HIGH_BASES = {'none', 'usage_valuation', 'proxy_charge', 'proxy_reservation'}
   42| PLACEMENTS = {'inside', 'before', 'after', 'straddles_start', 'straddles_closure', 'unplaceable', 'uncertain', 'not_evaluated'}
   43| OWNERSHIP = {'owned', 'uncertain', 'unadmitted', 'unowned'}
   44| FORWARDING = {'forwarded', 'unknown'}                 # NotForwarded requests are non-attempt evidence (contract 6.1)
   45| KEY_JOIN = {'exact', 'ambiguous', 'unattributed'}     # contract 4: the key join; RS rules are auxiliary rows
   46| SHAPE_RULES = {'RS-1', 'RS-2', 'RS-3'}
   47| EXECUTION = {'terminal', 'not_terminal'}
   48| BILLING = {'established', 'unresolved', 'not_applicable'}
   49| TERMINAL_EVENTS = {'request', 'upstream_error', 'stream_error', 'response_model_refused'}
   50| COMMIT_STATES = {'pending', 'launched_open', 'retired', 'retired_resumable', 'released', 'reopened', 'invalid'}
   51| OPEN_COMMIT = {'pending', 'launched_open', 'retired_resumable', 'reopened'}
   52| LIFE = {'open', 'retired', 'contradicted'}
   53| INVARIANTS = ['INV-F1', 'INV-F2', 'INV-F3', 'INV-F4', 'INV-F5', 'INV-F6', 'INV-F7', 'INV-F8', 'INV-F9', 'INV-F10', 'INV-F11', 'INV-F12', 'INV-F13',
   54|               'INV-T1', 'INV-T2', 'INV-T3', 'INV-T4', 'INV-T5', 'INV-A1', 'INV-A2', 'INV-A3', 'INV-A4', 'INV-A5', 'INV-A6', 'INV-A7', 'INV-A8', 'INV-A9', 'INV-A10']
   55| KEY = re.compile(r'^(res|row|adm):(S\d)/turn-(\d+):(rid|line)=(\d+)$')
   56| REF = re.compile(r'^(S\d)/turn-(\d+):(\d+)$')
   57| STAMP = re.compile(r'^\d{4}-\d\d-\d\dT\d\d:\d\d:\d\dZ$')
   58| MIN_BLOCKED = 25
   59| # revision 4 (OQ-1): admitted valid controls are counted per profile. Total minimum 15 is kept; the current producer
   60| # can only admit sessionless or Codex cases, so metered positives live in the future profile (no padding of either).
   61| MIN_ADMITTED = {'current_producer': 1, 'future_qualified_gate': 11}; MIN_VALID_ADMITTED = 15
   62| ADDED_IN = {'1.1-s1', '1.2-s1', '1.3-s1', '1.4-s1', '1.5-s1', '1.6-s1'}
   63| REPLACEMENT_REASONS = {'UsageBound', 'NotUsageSettlement', 'UsageIncomplete', 'UsageUnpriceable', 'NoReservation'}
   64| TIE = {'usage_valuation': 0, 'proxy_reservation': 1, 'proxy_charge': 2}     # X-0 (1.4-s1)
   65| COVERS = ['failures', 'forwarding', 'partial_streams', 'retries', 'settlement']
   66| PROVIDERS = {'openai': 'openai', 'openai-codex': 'openai', 'anthropic': 'anthropic'}
   67| RANK = {'admitted': 0, 'refused': 1, 'not_established': 1}     # OQ-2: refused and not_established are equally non-permissive
   68| VERTICAL = 'vertical_admission_authority'
   69| MECHS = ('new_forwarding', 'retries', 'reopening', 'billing_bound')
   70| 
   71| 
   72| class Failures(list):
   73|     def add(self, case, msg): self.append(f'{case}: {msg}')
   74| 
   75| 
   76| def nano(s):
   77|     if not isinstance(s, str) or not re.fullmatch(r'-?\d+', s): raise ValueError(f'not a nano integer string: {s!r}')
   78|     return int(s)
   79| 
   80| 
   81| def load_rates(rates_dir):
   82|     out = {}
   83|     for name in ('openai_api_rates.json', 'anthropic_api_rates.json'):
   84|         raw = open(os.path.join(rates_dir, name), 'rb').read()
   85|         out[name] = (hashlib.sha256(raw).hexdigest(), json.loads(raw, parse_float=Decimal, parse_int=Decimal))
   86|     return out
   87| 
   88| 
   89| def per_token(rate, mult=1):
   90|     v = Fraction(rate) * Fraction(mult) * Fraction(10 ** 9, 10 ** 6)
   91|     if v.denominator != 1: raise ValueError('rate not exact in nano per token')
   92|     return int(v)
   93| 
   94| 
   95| def price(d, rates):
   96|     """Arithmetic of one derivation record against the rate files (the contract's pricing primitives, 7.1)."""
   97|     u, m, mult = d['usage'], d['model'], Fraction(Decimal(d['tier_multiplier']))
   98|     if d['provider'] == 'openai':
   99|         oa = rates['openai_api_rates.json'][1]; r = oa['models'][m]
  100|         i, o = u['input_tokens'], u['output_tokens']; c, w = u.get('cached_tokens') or 0, u.get('cache_write_tokens') or 0
  101|         long_ = i > int(oa['long_input_threshold'])
  102|         mi = Fraction(oa['long_input_multiplier']) if long_ else 1; mo = Fraction(oa['long_output_multiplier']) if long_ else 1
  103|         return ((i - c - w) * per_token(r['input'], mult * mi) + c * per_token(r['cached_input'], mult * mi)
  104|                 + w * per_token(r['cache_write'], mult * mi) + o * per_token(r['output'], mult * mo))
  105|     an = rates['anthropic_api_rates.json'][1]; r = an['models'][m]
  106|     i, cr, cw, o = (u.get(k, 0) for k in ('input_tokens', 'cache_read_input_tokens', 'cache_creation_input_tokens', 'output_tokens'))
  107|     w5, w1 = u.get('ephemeral_5m_input_tokens') or 0, u.get('ephemeral_1h_input_tokens') or 0
  108|     rate = (max if d.get('remainder_rate', 'max') == 'max' else min)(per_token(r['cache_write_5m']), per_token(r['cache_write_1h']))
  109|     writes = cw * rate if d.get('split') == 'ignored' else (w5 * per_token(r['cache_write_5m']) + w1 * per_token(r['cache_write_1h']) + (cw - w5 - w1) * rate)
  110|     return i * per_token(r['input']) + cr * per_token(r['cache_read']) + writes + o * per_token(r['output'])
  111| 
  112| 
  113| def hsum(items):
  114|     """Sum of (low, kind, high) exposures with AtLeast propagation (contract 2)."""
  115|     low = sum(x[0] for x in items); high = sum(x[2] for x in items)
  116|     return low, ('at_least' if any(x[1] == 'at_least' for x in items) else 'exact'), high
  117| 
  118| 
  119| def esum_of(e): return (nano(e['low']), e['high']['kind'], nano(e['high']['nano']))
  120| 
  121| 
  122| def att_exp(a):
  123|     x = a['exposure']
  124|     if x['pool'] == 'codex': return None
  125|     return (nano(x['low']['nano']), x['high']['kind'], nano(x['high']['nano']))
  126| 
  127| 
  128| def parse(line):
  129|     try: row = json.loads(line) if isinstance(line, str) else None
  130|     except ValueError: return None
  131|     return row if isinstance(row, dict) else None
  132| 
  133| 
  134| def source_lines(c):
  135|     """{ref: raw line} and {source: [refs in append order]}."""
  136|     lines, order = {}, {}
  137|     for s in c['inputs']['sessions']:
  138|         for turn, log in s['logs'].items():
  139|             src = f'{s["alias"]}/{turn}'
  140|             for n, ln in enumerate(log['lines'], 1):
  141|                 lines[f'{src}:{n}'] = ln; order.setdefault(src, []).append(f'{src}:{n}')
  142|     return lines, order
  143| 
  144| 
  145| def forwarded_row(line):
  146|     """Contract 6.1 (1.2-s1): a provider response exists."""
  147|     row = parse(line)
  148|     if row is None: return False
  149|     if row.get('event') in ('response_model_refused', 'stream_error'): return True
  150|     if row.get('event') != 'request': return False
  151|     st = row.get('http_status')
  152|     if isinstance(st, int) and not isinstance(st, bool): return True
  153|     if row.get('response_id') or row.get('response_model'): return True
  154|     u = row.get('usage')
  155|     return isinstance(u, dict) and any(v is not None for v in u.values())
  156| 
  157| 
  158| def reopened_of(session):
  159|     """Contract L-5: a deliver follows a retire anywhere in the lifecycle rows."""
  160|     seen = False
  161|     for r in session['lifecycle']:
  162|         if r['event'] == 'retire': seen = True
  163|         elif r['event'] == 'deliver' and seen: return True
  164|     return False
  165| 
  166| 
  167| def closure_state(o, ref, profile='current_producer'):
  168|     """Contract 12.4 step 1 (1.3-s1): 'open', 'closed' or 'claimed' (claimed but not established)."""
  169|     ca, ce, cr = o.get('closed_at'), o.get('close'), o.get('closure_record')
  170|     if ca is None and ce is None and cr is None: return 'open'
  171|     rows = [r for a in o.get('admissions', []) for r in a['rows']]
  172|     coherent = isinstance(ca, str) and STAMP.match(ca) is not None and ca >= o['started_at'] and ca <= ref and \
  173|         not any(isinstance(r.get('at'), str) and r['at'] > ca for r in rows)
  174|     if profile == 'current_producer':      # legacy cooperative trust: a consistent scope_budget close entry
  175|         ok = coherent and isinstance(ce, dict) and ce.get('at') == ca and cr is None
  176|     else:                                  # future vertical: the objective's declared admission authority
  177|         auth = o.get('admission_authority') or {}
  178|         ok = coherent and isinstance(cr, dict) and auth.get('kind') == VERTICAL and cr.get('authority_id') == auth.get('id') and \
  179|             cr.get('at') == ca and isinstance(cr.get('receipt'), dict) and cr['receipt'].get('ref') and re.fullmatch(r'[0-9a-f]{64}', str(cr['receipt'].get('sha256')))
  180|     return 'closed' if ok else 'claimed'
  181| 
  182| 
  183| def gate_qualification(c, o, adm, purpose, ref, lifecycle_by_sid, sessions_by_sid, cap_nano=None):
  184|     """Contract 2.3 G-1..G-7 for one admission's session. purpose: 'release' (retired) or 'bound' (open/pending).
  185|     Returns (qualified, reasons, contradicted); a qualified 'bound' gate also sets gate_qualification.ceiling."""
  186|     gate_qualification.ceiling = None
  187|     gid = adm.get('gate_id')
  188|     if not gid: return False, ['NoGateEvidence'], False
  189|     g = next((x for x in c['inputs'].get('gates', []) if x.get('gate_id') == gid), None)
  190|     if not isinstance(g, dict) or not all(k in g for k in ('authority', 'session', 'scope', 'effective_from', 'effective_until', 'writer', 'mechanisms')):
  191|         return False, ['GateMalformed'], False
  192|     reasons = []; auth = o.get('admission_authority') or {}
  193|     witness = c.get('trusted_fixture_inputs', {}).get('gate_verifications', {}).get(gid)
  194|     payload = hashlib.sha256(json.dumps(g, sort_keys=True, separators=(',', ':'), ensure_ascii=False).encode()).hexdigest()
  195|     if not isinstance(witness, dict): reasons.append('GateVerificationMissing')
  196|     elif witness.get('source') != 'test_mock' or not witness.get('policy_ref') or not witness.get('verification_evidence'):
  197|         reasons.append('GateVerificationRejected')
  198|     elif witness.get('payload_sha256') != payload or witness.get('authority_id') != (g.get('authority') or {}).get('id') or witness.get('session') != g.get('session'):
  199|         reasons.append('GateVerificationBinding')
  200|     ceiling = None
  201|     if g['writer'] != 'authority': reasons.append('GateWriterNotAuthority')
  202|     if (g['authority'] or {}).get('kind') != VERTICAL or (g['authority'] or {}).get('id') != auth.get('id') or auth.get('kind') != VERTICAL: reasons.append('GateAuthorityMismatch')
  203|     if g['session'] != adm['session']: reasons.append('GateSessionMismatch')
  204|     if (g['scope'] or {}).get('kind') != 'session': reasons.append('GateScopeInsufficient')
  205|     launched = [r.get('at') for r in adm['rows'] if r.get('event') in ('launched', 'objective_admission') and isinstance(r.get('at'), str)]
  206|     if not (isinstance(g['effective_from'], str) and STAMP.match(g['effective_from']) and launched and g['effective_from'] <= min(launched)) or \
  207|             not (g['effective_until'] is None or (isinstance(g['effective_until'], str) and g['effective_until'] >= ref)):
  208|         reasons.append('GatePeriodInsufficient')
  209|     need = ('new_forwarding', 'retries', 'reopening') if purpose == 'release' else ('billing_bound', 'retries')
  210|     m = g['mechanisms'] or {}
  211|     if not all((m.get(k) or {}).get('enforced') is True for k in need): reasons.append('GateMechanismMissing')
  212|     if any((m.get(k) or {}).get('enforced') is True and not ((m[k].get('receipt') or {}).get('ref') and re.fullmatch(r'[0-9a-f]{64}', str((m[k].get('receipt') or {}).get('sha256'))))
  213|            for k in need): reasons.append('GateReceiptMissing')
  214|     if purpose == 'bound' and (m.get('billing_bound') or {}).get('enforced') is True:   # G-8 (1.4-s1, A-03)
  215|         bnd = m['billing_bound'].get('bound')
  216|         if not isinstance(bnd, dict) or not (isinstance(bnd.get('amount_nano'), str) and re.fullmatch(r'\d+', bnd['amount_nano'])): reasons.append('GateBoundMissing')
  217|         else:
  218|             if cap_nano is None or int(bnd['amount_nano']) > cap_nano: reasons.append('GateBoundExceedsCap')
  219|             if bnd.get('currency') != 'USD': reasons.append('GateBoundCurrency')
  220|             if bnd.get('scope') != 'session' or sorted(bnd.get('covers') or []) != COVERS: reasons.append('GateBoundCoverage')
  221|             doc = {'amount_nano': bnd['amount_nano'], 'authority_id': (g['authority'] or {}).get('id'), 'covers': sorted(bnd.get('covers') or []), 'currency': bnd.get('currency'),
  222|                    'effective_from': g['effective_from'], 'effective_until': g['effective_until'], 'gate_id': gid, 'session': g['session']}
  223|             if bnd.get('binding_sha256') != hashlib.sha256(json.dumps(doc, sort_keys=True, separators=(',', ':')).encode()).hexdigest(): reasons.append('GateBoundBinding')
  224|             ceiling = int(bnd['amount_nano'])
  225|     contradicted = False
  226|     sess = sessions_by_sid.get(adm['session'])
  227|     if sess is not None and (m.get('reopening') or {}).get('enforced') is True:
  228|         seen = False
  229|         for r in sess['lifecycle']:
  230|             if r['event'] == 'retire': seen = True
  231|             elif r['event'] == 'deliver' and seen and isinstance(r.get('at'), str) and isinstance(g['effective_from'], str) and r['at'] >= g['effective_from']:
  232|                 contradicted = True
  233|     if contradicted: reasons.append('GateContradicted')
  234|     if not reasons: gate_qualification.ceiling = ceiling
  235|     return (not reasons), reasons, contradicted
  236| 
  237| 
  238| def gate_projection(c, adm, qualified, reasons):
  239|     out = {'qualified': qualified, 'reasons': reasons}
